<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For SL<sub><i>n</i></sub> in characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; <i>n</i>, we construct nonzero locally constructible perverse Hecke eigensheaves with Borel level at two or more marked points on a smooth projective curve of genus at least two over an algebraic closure of a finite field. The parameter is a Zariski-dense geometric <i>ℓ</i>-adic PGL<sub><i>n</i></sub>-local system with unipotent tame monodromy; its nilpotent logarithms may have any Jordan type, including zero. The eigensheaves have parabolic nilpotent singular support, and their eigenisomorphisms retain the full tensor and fusion structure.

- **claim-main-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-main)): For every parameter <a href="#intro:parameter" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:parameter">[intro:parameter]</a> satisfying the preceding hypotheses, there is a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric $E$-adic sheaf $M$ on $\mathcal A$ with $$\relax(M)\subset\Lambda_D.$$ For every finite set $I$ and every family $(V_i)_{i\in I}$, it has isomorphisms $$\label{intro:eigen} \mathop{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{I,(V_i)}(M) \simeq M\boxtimes\Bigl(\mathop{\boxtimes}_{i\in I}(V_i)_\rho\Bigr) \qquad\text{on }\mathcal A\times U^I,$$ natural in the representations and coherent for the tensor unit, convolution, permutations, and fusion on every collision diagonal. These isomorphisms use the relative normalization above.
  - Explanation: For every admissible parameter there is a nonzero locally constructible perverse eigensheaf on the Borel-level bundle stack. Its singular support lies in the cone of generically nilpotent Higgs fields, and its Hecke eigenisomorphisms are natural and coherent.
- **claim-nearby-cycles-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-nearby)): For finite-type $S$-schemes, geometric nearby cycles preserve bounded constructibility, are perverse exact, commute with smooth pullback and proper pushforward, and satisfy exterior-product comparison. They are unchanged by finite extensions of the trait inside the fixed geometric generic field. The same statements hold locally on the smooth stacks used here, with representable proper maps.* *Let $Z$ be a finite-type $S$-scheme. Suppose a lisse finite-rank sheaf $L_{\bar\eta}$ on $Z_{\bar\eta}$ has a lattice over the integers of a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$. Assume that each finite reduction extends lisse over $Z$ after a finite extension of $S$, compatibly on further common extensions, with special reductions belonging to a fixed lisse sheaf $L_0$ on $Z_0$. The trait extension may depend on the reduction. Then for any map $g:Y\to Z$ of finite-type $S$-schemes and any bounded constructible $F$ on $Y_{\bar\eta}$, $$\label{found:lattice} \Psi_YF\otimes g_0^*L_0 \xrightarrow{\sim} \Psi_Y(F\otimes g_{\bar\eta}^*L_{\bar\eta}).$$ This is natural in maps and tensor products of the lisse systems, and holds locally on the smooth stacks in use.
  - Explanation: Geometric nearby cycles preserve constructibility and perversity and commute with smooth pullback and proper pushforward. They also satisfy a tensor formula against lisse systems that have compatible lattices.
- **claim-hecke-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-hecke-specialization)): Let $\mathcal B/S$ be smooth and locally of finite type, and let $\mathscr U/S$ be a smooth curve of allowed legs. Assume the split spherical disc models, proper bounds, and smooth exact-position maps described in Section <a href="#found:level-stacks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:level-stacks">2.1</a>. Then there are natural comparisons $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hecke}}^0_{I,\boldsymbol V}(\Psi F) \xrightarrow{\sim} \Psi_{\mathcal B\times_S\mathscr U^I} \mathop{\mathrm{Hecke}}^{\bar\eta}_{I,\boldsymbol V}(F) \qquad(F\in D_{\mathrm{lcc}}(\mathcal B_{\bar\eta},E)),$$ compatible with the entire relative Hecke system of Definition <a href="#found:coherence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:coherence">2</a>. Consequently, if $F$ has a coherent lisse eigenvalue whose lattices satisfy Proposition <a href="#found:nearby" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:nearby">3</a> compatibly as a tensor system, $\Psi F$ has the specialized coherent eigenvalue.
  - Explanation: Nearby cycles commute with the relative Hecke functors. A coherent eigenstructure with lisse eigenvalue therefore specializes to a coherent eigenstructure on the special fibre.
- **claim-cone-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-dimension)): For a smooth finite-type chart $S\to\mathcal B$ of pure dimension $d$, $$\dim\Lambda_S\le d.$$ In characteristic zero the cone is isotropic: the symplectic form vanishes on the smooth locus of every reduced subvariety of $\Lambda_S$.
  - Explanation: On a smooth chart the generically nilpotent cone has dimension at most that of the chart. In characteristic zero it is isotropic for the symplectic form.
- **claim-detecting-cocharacter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-cocharacter)): If $a(t)\in\mathfrak{sl}_n[[t]]$ and $a(0)$ is not nilpotent, then, after a change of formal $\mathrm{SL}_n$-frame, there are a block Levi $M$ and a cocharacter $\lambda:\mathbb G_m\to Z(M)$ such that $$\label{geom:normal-form} a(t)\in\mathfrak m[[t]],\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{ad}}(a(0))\|_{\mathfrak g/\mathfrak m}\text{ is invertible},\qquad \kappa(a(0),d\lambda)\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: A non-nilpotent leading term can be put in a block Levi after a change of frame. A central cocharacter of that Levi then pairs nontrivially with it.
- **claim-transverse-modification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-transverse)): For a geometric covector $A\in T_b^*\mathcal B\setminus\Lambda$, there are an allowed leg $y$, such an $H$, a point $h$ with $p(h)=b$ and $l(h)=y$, a covector $A'\in T^*_{o(h)}\mathcal B$, and $0\ne\xi\in T_y^*U$ with $$\label{geom:identity} p^*A=q_H^*(A',\xi)\quad\text{at }h.$$ The following sections have nondegenerate zeros at $h$:* 1. *on $H_{\mathrm{in}}=\widetilde p^{-1}(b,y)$, the restriction of $p^*A$ to the $q_H$-vertical tangent bundle;* 2. *on $H_{\mathrm{out}}=q_H^{-1}(o(h),y)$, the restriction of $o^*A'$ to the $\widetilde p$-vertical tangent bundle.* *Each restriction is a section of the dual of the indicated vertical tangent bundle. The fibers include the identifications of the fixed bundles; these fibers and the vertical tangent bundles have dimension and rank $m$, respectively. The point $y$ can be chosen in any prescribed nonempty open of a component on which $A$ is not generically nilpotent.
  - Explanation: A covector outside the nilpotent cone yields a Hecke modification at some leg. The two relevant vertical sections have nondegenerate zeros at the modification point.
- **claim-field-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-field)): Let $F\in D_{\mathrm{lcc}}(\mathcal B)$ be a Hecke eigencomplex with lisse eigenvalues. In the product and quotient cases assume the eigenrelations on both open curves. Then, for every smooth finite-type chart $b:S\to\mathcal B$, $$\relax(b^*F)\subset\Lambda_S.$$ For this conclusion it is enough to have the individual one-leg isomorphisms $\mathop{\mathrm{Hecke}}_V(F)\simeq F\boxtimes\mathcal V_V$ for all Satake representations $V$, with each $\mathcal V_V$ lisse; their coherence is not needed in this section.
  - Explanation: A Hecke eigencomplex with lisse eigenvalues has singular support inside the generically nilpotent cone on every smooth chart. Only the one-leg eigenisomorphisms are needed, not their coherence.
- **claim-specialization-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-specialization)): In this relative setup, let $F\in D_{\mathrm{lcc}}(\mathcal B_{\bar\eta})$ be a Hecke eigencomplex with lisse eigenvalues on $L_{\bar\eta}$ satisfying the lattice specialization condition of Proposition <a href="#found:nearby" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:nearby">3</a>. If $\Psi F=0$, then on every smooth finite-type chart $D\to\mathcal B$ the closure of the generic nonzero-stalk locus of $F\|_{D_{\bar\eta}}$ does not meet $D_s$.
  - Explanation: If nearby cycles of such an eigencomplex vanish, the closure of its generic nonzero-stalk locus misses the special fibre. This is the nonvanishing criterion used in both specializations.
- **claim-hypersurface-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-cut)): If the differential $df_d$ in the field case, respectively its relative special-fiber differential in the trait case, lies outside $\Lambda_D$, then $$(\Phi_f K)_d=0 \quad\text{in the field case},\qquad \bigl(\Psi(K\|_{\{f=0\}_{\bar\eta}})\bigr)_d=0 \quad\text{in the trait case}.$$ Here $\Phi_f$ is the unshifted cone from restriction to geometric nearby cycles for $f$.
  - Explanation: If the differential of a cutting function avoids the nilpotent cone, the relevant vanishing or nearby cycles vanish at the point. The proof uses two Hecke modifications and a split quadric.
- **claim-simultaneous-cuts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-simultaneous)): In the trait case, assume $\Psi F=0$. Let $f_1,\ldots,f_r$ vanish at $d\in D_s$ and suppose their special-fiber differentials span an $r$-dimensional subspace $V\subset T_d^*D_s$ with $V\cap(\Lambda_D)_d=\{0\}$. Then nearby cycles of $K\|_{\{f_1=\cdots=f_r=0\}_{\bar\eta}}$ vanish at $d$. The statement for $r=0$ is $\Psi K=0$.
  - Explanation: Several cutting functions whose differentials span a subspace missing the cone also give vanishing nearby cycles. An incidence variety handles all the cuts at once.
- **claim-perverse-eigen** (theorem, [source](#stmt-perv-eigen)): Suppose $F\in D_{\mathrm{lcc}}(\mathcal B,E)$ is a coherent Hecke eigencomplex for a continuous parameter $\sigma:\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(U)\to\check G(L)$ with Zariski-dense image, where $L/\mathbb Q_\ell$ is finite. Then every ${}^pH^j(F)$ has a natural coherent eigenstructure for $\sigma$, with singular support in $\Lambda$. The eigenmaps are natural in representations and compatible with the unit, convolution, permutations, and fusion on every collision diagonal. If $F\ne0$, some ${}^pH^j(F)$ is nonzero. No global cohomological bound on $F$ and no condition on the boundary monodromy are required.
  - Explanation: Each perverse cohomology of a dense coherent eigencomplex again has a coherent eigenstructure with singular support in the cone. A nonzero eigencomplex has a nonzero perverse cohomology.
- **claim-scalar-spectral-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-perv-scalar)): Let $K\in\mathscr D_\Lambda(\mathcal B)$ have a coherent Betti eigenstructure with parameter represented by $y\in Y(E)$. Every $f\in E[Y]^{\check G}$ acts, as a degree-zero endomorphism of the spectral unit and hence of $K$, by $f(y)\operatorname{id}_K$.
  - Explanation: Invariant functions on the parameter space act on the eigencomplex by scalars given by their value at the parameter.
- **claim-equivariant-frame** (theorem, [source](#stmt-perv-frame)): If the entries of $y\in Y(E)$ generate a Zariski-dense subgroup of $\check G=\mathrm{PGL}_{n,E}$, then for every nonzero representation $V$, of dimension $b$, there are an invariant $f_V\in E[Y]^{\check G}$ with $f_V(y)\ne0$ and equivariant maps $$\alpha_V:\mathcal O_Y^{\oplus b}\longrightarrow\mathscr E_V, \qquad \beta_V:\mathscr E_V\longrightarrow\mathcal O_Y^{\oplus b}$$ such that $$\label{perv:adjugates} \beta_V\alpha_V=f_V\operatorname{id},\qquad \alpha_V\beta_V=f_V\operatorname{id}.$$
  - Explanation: For a dense parameter each tautological bundle is trivialized by equivariant maps after inverting an invariant function that is nonzero at the parameter. The maps are inverse up to that function.
- **claim-fixed-point-exactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-perv-exact)): The subcategory $\mathscr D_S$ is preserved by perverse truncations and by all fixed-point Hecke functors. Every fixed-point Hecke functor is perverse $t$-exact on this subcategory.
  - Explanation: The localized subcategory is stable under perverse truncation and fixed-point Hecke functors, and these functors are t-exact on it.
- **claim-monodromic-torsor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-monodromic)): If $Q\in D_{\mathrm{lcc}}(\mathcal A^+,E)$ has nilpotent singular support, its Betti realization is monodromic along the whole $T^s$-torsor $q$, and along each enhancement separately.
  - Explanation: Nilpotent singular support forces the Betti realization to be monodromic along the enhancement torus torsor and along each enhancement separately.
- **claim-torus-exchange** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-exchange)): Let $P\xrightarrow{\pi}C\xrightarrow{b}Y$ be a factorization over an analytic disc $\Delta$, with $\pi$ a torus torsor on the entire family and $b$ proper. On finite-dimensional paracompact charts suppose that $F$ over $\Delta^*$ is weakly constructible and relatively monodromic along $\pi$. Then the natural exchange $$\label{desc:push-exchange} \Psi(b\pi)_*F\longrightarrow(b_0\pi_0)_*\Psi F$$ is an isomorphism. Base change and projection formula for these pushforwards also hold, and the comparisons respect composition. Here $\Psi$ denotes geometric Betti nearby cycles, without taking monodromy invariants.
  - Explanation: Nearby cycles commute with pushforward through a torus torsor followed by a proper map when the sheaf is relatively monodromic along the torsor.
- **claim-central-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-trace)): Let $Q$ be a locally bounded algebraically constructible Betti complex on $\mathcal A^+$, monodromic along $T^s$, with coherent Hecke eigenisomorphisms for $\sigma$ on $U$. The local central kernels at $x_i$ act on $Q$, and there are natural isomorphisms $$\label{desc:central-eigen} Z(V)\star_{x_i}Q\simeq Q\otimes_E V_{\partial i}$$ compatible with the unit, representation morphisms, and convolution. On each individual convolution factor they carry $m_V$ to $M_{V,i}$. In particular, for every $i$ and every $V\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}(\check G)$, $$\label{desc:character-trace} \chi_V(\mu_i)=\operatorname{Tr}(M_{V,i})\,\mathrm{id}_Q.$$
  - Explanation: The local central kernels at one marked point act on the eigencomplex through the boundary fibre. The character of the enhancement action equals the trace of the boundary monodromy.
- **claim-ordinary-level-descent** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-desc-ordinary)): Let $\sigma$ be a continuous Zariski-dense geometric adic parameter on $U$, defined over a finite coefficient extension, with unipotent local monodromy at every $x_i$. If $\mathcal A^+$ carries a nonzero lcc geometric adic coherent Hecke eigencomplex for $\sigma$, then $\mathcal A$ carries a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric adic Hecke eigensheaf for $\sigma$. It has all the unit, convolution, permutation, and fusion compatibilities of Definition <a href="#found:coherence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:coherence">2</a>, and nilpotent singular support. No boundary monodromy is required to be regular.
  - Explanation: Given a nonzero coherent eigencomplex on the enhanced stack, unipotent boundary monodromy forces unipotent torus monodromy. Compactly supported pushforward then gives a nonzero perverse eigensheaf on the ordinary-flag stack.
- **claim-unramified-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-unramified)): (). *Let $C$ be a smooth projective connected curve over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $G$ be simple and simply connected. A continuous $\widehat G$-local system $\tau$ on $C$, defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$ and with Zariski-dense image, admits a nonzero locally bounded constructible geometric $E$-adic eigencomplex on $\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(C)$. Its Hecke eigenstructure has the relative Satake normalization and is coherent for all finite sets of legs, including collision maps.
  - Explanation: In characteristic zero a dense unramified parameter has a nonzero locally bounded constructible eigencomplex on the unlevelled bundle stack, with coherent Hecke structure.
- **claim-nodal-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-quotient)): There is an equivalence $$\label{con:special-quotient} (\mathcal B_m)_0\simeq [(\mathcal A_1^+\times\mathcal A_2^+)/T^s].$$ The $i$th torus changes simultaneously the two frames at the $i$th gluing gerbe, expressed using its common node generator. A Hecke modification in $U_j$ pulls back to the usual modification on the $j$th factor.
  - Explanation: The special fibre of the twisted bundle stack is the quotient of the two enhanced stacks by the simultaneous action of the product of tori at the nodes.
- **claim-smoothing-parameter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-parameter)): Put $K_1=\overline{\mathbb C((z))}$ and let $C_{K_1}$ be the smooth geometric generic curve. There is a continuous unramified parameter $\rho_{K_1}$ on $C_{K_1}$ with the same compact image as $\rho_1$. Its lattice reductions specialize on $U_1\sqcup U_2$ to those of $\rho_1$ and $\rho_2$, with the tensor compatibilities of Proposition <a href="#found:hecke-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:hecke-specialization">4</a>.
  - Explanation: The parameter and its orientation-reversed copy glue to an unramified parameter on the geometric generic curve of the smoothing, with the same compact image.
- **claim-charzero-eigensheaf** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-charzero)): Let $X/\mathbb C$ be a smooth projective connected curve of genus at least two, with $s\geq2$ distinct marked points, divisor $D$ their sum, and $G=\mathrm{SL}_n$. Let $\sigma$ be a continuous dense $\check G$-parameter on their complement, defined over a finite coefficient extension, with unipotent monodromy at every marking. Then $\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_{G,B,D}(X)$ carries a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric $E$-adic eigensheaf with eigenvalue $\sigma$, nilpotent singular support, and the full coherent Hecke system in relative Satake normalization.
  - Explanation: Over the complex numbers the Borel-level stack at several marked points carries a nonzero locally constructible perverse eigensheaf for any dense parameter with unipotent monodromy, with nilpotent singular support.
- **claim-parameter-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lift-parameter)): The parameter $\rho$ lifts to a continuous parameter $\rho_{K_0}:\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(\mathscr U_{K_0})\to\check G(L)$ with the same compact image. After compatible choices of boundary fibers and prime-to-$p$ roots of unity, its full inertia homomorphism at $\mathfrak x_i$ is $$\label{lift:inertia} \rho_{K_0}(\gamma)=\exp\bigl(t_{\ell,i}(\gamma)N_i\bigr).$$ In particular every other prime factor of characteristic-zero inertia acts trivially. Its lattice reductions extend lisse over $\mathscr U$ and specialize to those of $\rho$, with the tensor compatibilities of Proposition <a href="#found:hecke-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:hecke-specialization">4</a>.
  - Explanation: The tame parameter lifts to characteristic zero with the same compact image and the same full inertia homomorphisms. Its lattice reductions specialize compatibly.

      "exact_statement": "For a geometric covector $A\\in T_b^*\\mathcal B\\setminus\\Lambda$, there are an allowed leg $y$, such an $H$, a point $h$ with $p(h)=b$ and $l(h)=y$, a covector $A'\\in T^*_{o(h)}\\mathcal B$, and $0\\ne\\xi\\in T_y^*U$ with $$\\label{geom:identity}\n                         p^*A=q_H^*(A',\\xi)\\quad\\text{at }h.$$ The following sections have nondegenerate zeros at $h$:*\n\n1.  *on $H_{\\mathrm{in}}=\\widetilde p^{-1}(b,y)$, the restriction of $p^*A$ to the $q_H$-vertical tangent bundle;*\n\n2.  *on $H_{\\mathrm{out}}=q_H^{-1}(o(h),y)$, the restriction of $o^*A'$ to the $\\widetilde p$-vertical tangent bundle.*\n\n*Each restriction is a section of the dual of the indicated vertical tangent bundle. The fibers include the identifications of the fixed bundles; these fibers and the vertical tangent bundles have dimension and rank $m$, respectively. The point $y$ can be chosen in any prescribed nonempty open of a component on which $A$ is not generically nilpotent.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-perv-eigen",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $F\\in D_{\\mathrm{lcc}}(\\mathcal B,E)$ is a coherent Hecke eigencomplex for a continuous parameter $\\sigma:\\pi_1^{\\mathrm{et}}(U)\\to\\check G(L)$ with Zariski-dense image, where $L/\\mathbb Q_\\ell$ is finite. Then every ${}^pH^j(F)$ has a natural coherent eigenstructure for $\\sigma$, with singular support in $\\Lambda$. The eigenmaps are natural in representations and compatible with the unit, convolution, permutations, and fusion on every collision diagonal. If $F\\ne0$, some ${}^pH^j(F)$ is nonzero. No global cohomological bound on $F$ and no condition on the boundary monodromy are required.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.7"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-desc-exchange",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P\\xrightarrow{\\pi}C\\xrightarrow{b}Y$ be a factorization over an analytic disc $\\Delta$, with $\\pi$ a torus torsor on the entire family and $b$ proper. On finite-dimensional paracompact charts suppose that $F$ over $\\Delta^*$ is weakly constructible and relatively monodromic along $\\pi$. Then the natural exchange $$\\label{desc:push-exchange}\n            \\Psi(b\\pi)_*F\\longrightarrow(b_0\\pi_0)_*\\Psi F$$ is an isomorphism. Base change and projection formula for these pushforwards also hold, and the comparisons respect composition. Here $\\Psi$ denotes geometric Betti nearby cycles, without taking monodromy invariants.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.7"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-desc-exchange",

Then the natural exchange $$\\label{desc:push-exchange}\n            \\Psi(b\\pi)_*F\\longrightarrow(b_0\\pi_0)_*\\Psi F$$ is an isomorphism. Base change and projection formula for these pushforwards also hold, and the comparisons respect composition. Here $\\Psi$ denotes geometric Betti nearby cycles, without taking monodromy invariants.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.7"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $P\\xrightarrow{\\pi}C\\xrightarrow{b}Y$ be a factorization over an analytic disc $\\Delta$, with $\\pi$ a torus torsor on the entire family and $b$ proper.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-desc-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be a locally bounded algebraically constructible Betti complex on $\\mathcal A^+$, monodromic along $T^s$, with coherent Hecke eigenisomorphisms for $\\sigma$ on $U$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **locally constructible perverse complex on a stack** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A geometric $E$-adic complex $M$ on $\mathcal A$ is *locally constructible perverse* if $f^*M[d]$ is bounded constructible and perverse for every smooth finite-type chart $f:S\to\mathcal A$ of constant relative dimension $d$.
- **cone of generically nilpotent Higgs fields with level** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $\Lambda_D$ be the cone of these fields that are nilpotent at the generic point of $X$.
- **relative Satake normalization** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We use relative Satake normalization: on a Schubert cell of dimension $d_\lambda$ the kernel is $E[d_\lambda](d_\lambda/2)$, and there is no additional moving-leg shift.
- **coherent eigenstructure** ([source](#stmt-found-coherence)): A coherent eigenstructure for a tensor local system $V\mapsto L_V$ on $U$ consists of natural isomorphisms $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{I,\boldsymbol V}(F) \simeq F\boxtimes\Bigl(\mathop{\boxtimes}_{i\in I}L_{V_i}\Bigr)$$ for all finite sets and representations, compatible with the unit, successive modifications and convolution, permutations, and fusion.
- **generically nilpotent cone** ([source](#stmt-geom-cone)): For any of these stacks $\mathcal B$, let $\Lambda\subset T^*\mathcal B$ be the cone of Higgs fields generically nilpotent on each component curve in its presentation. For a smooth chart $f:S\to\mathcal B$, write $\Lambda_S=f^\circ\Lambda$.
- **locally bounded constructible complexes** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a smooth stack $\mathcal B$ locally of finite type, $D_{\mathrm{lcc}}(\mathcal B,E)$ denotes the geometric adic complexes whose ordinary pullback to every smooth finite-type scheme chart is bounded constructible.
- **enhanced flag** ([source](#found:level-stacks)): We call the latter an enhanced flag.
- **monodromic along a torsor** ([source](#pm-h-24)): A complex on an $H$-torsor is *monodromic along the torsor* if, locally on its base, it is constructible for a product stratification $\{S_\alpha\times H\}$ and, on a contractible patch of $H$, is pulled back from a slice.

      "interpretation": "The conic subset of the cotangent stack consisting of Higgs fields that are generically nilpotent on each component curve, with its pullback to charts.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "generically nilpotent cone",
      "scope": "The level stacks of the geometry section: unlevelled, ordinary-flag, enhanced-flag, product and quotient stacks."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-8",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-8",
          "exact_quote": "For a smooth stack $\\mathcal B$ locally of finite type, $D_{\\mathrm{lcc}}(\\mathcal B,E)$ denotes the geometric adic complexes whose ordinary pullback to every smooth finite-type scheme chart is bounded constructible.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.7"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For a smooth stack $\\mathcal B$ locally of finite type, $D_{\\mathrm{lcc}}(\\mathcal B,E)$ denotes the geometric adic complexes whose ordinary pullback to every smooth finite-type scheme chart is bounded constructible.",
      "interpretation": "Complexes whose pullback to each smooth finite-type chart is bounded constructible, with bounds that may depend on the chart.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "locally bounded constructible complexes",
      "scope": "Smooth stacks locally of finite type with geometric adic coefficients."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "found:level-stacks",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "found:level-stacks",
          "exact_quote": "We call the latter an enhanced flag.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.7"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "We call the latter an enhanced flag.",
      "interpretation": "A reduction of the bundle to the unipotent radical at a marked point; forgetting it is a torsor under the torus.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "enhanced flag",
      "scope": "The level stacks over a curve with reduced divisor."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-24",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-24",
          "exact_quote": "A complex on an $H$-torsor is *monodromic along the torsor* if, locally on its base, it is constructible for a product stratification $\\{S_\\alpha\\times H\\}$ and, on a contractible patch of $H$, is pulled back from a slice.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.7"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A complex on an $H$-torsor is *monodromic along the torsor* if, locally on its base, it is constructible for a product stratification $\\{S_\\alpha\\times H\\}$ and, on a contractible patch of $H$, is pulled back from a slice.",
      "interpretation": "Local product constructibility along the torus fibres, allowing arbitrary monodromy but no equivariant structure.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "monodromic along a torsor",
      "scope": "Complexes on the enhanced stack over the torus torsor."
    }
  ],

}

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-genus-bound | genus of the curve | ≥ | $2$ | genus g |
| q-marked-points | number of marked points | ≥ | $2$ | s |
| q-characteristic | characteristic relative to the rank | > | $n$ | p and n |
| q-cone-dimension | dimension of the nilpotent cone on a chart | ≤ | $d$ | d |
| q-hecke-relative-dimension | relative dimension of the exact-position Hecke maps | = | $\langle2\rho_G,\lambda^+\rangle$ | m |
| q-satake-degree | dimension of the Schubert cell for a dominant coweight | = | $\langle 2\rho_G,\lambda\rangle$ | d_lambda |
| q-fundamental-group-rank | number of free generators of the topological fundamental group of the open curve | = | $2g+s-1$ | a |
| q-cone-total-dimension | dimension of the nilpotent cone on a relative chart | ≤ | $N$ | N |
| q-cone-fibre-dimension | dimension of the cone fibre at a general point of the special component | ≤ | $N-j$ | N and j |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-nearby-cycles** [claim](#stmt-found-nearby) / [proof](#stmt-found-nearby): Recall perverse exactness and constructibility of geometric nearby cycles and the lisse tensor formula -> Specialization functor with the properties needed for eigensheaves
- **r-hecke-specialization** [claim](#stmt-found-hecke-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-hecke-specialization): Exterior-product comparison in split local models, then successive steps and collisions -> Specialization of coherent eigenstructures
- **r-cone-dimension** [claim](#stmt-geom-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-dimension): Rational Borel reduction making the nilpotent field strictly triangular, then isotropy -> Dimension bound and isotropy of the cone
- **r-cocharacter** [claim](#stmt-geom-cocharacter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-cocharacter): Jordan decomposition, block centralizer, central cocharacter, inductive removal of off-block terms -> Normal form and detecting cocharacter
- **r-transverse** [claim](#stmt-geom-transverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-transverse): Modify by the cocharacter and compute tangent spaces and the perfect residue pairing -> Two nondegenerate zeros of the vertical sections
- **r-hypersurface-cut** [claim](#stmt-det-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-cut): Two Hecke modifications, isolation of one stalk by proper pushforward, split quadric compactification -> Vanishing of vanishing or nearby cycles for non-characteristic cuts
- **r-field-detection** [claim](#stmt-det-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-cut-2): Apply the hypersurface calculation on every chart and use the function-test description of singular support -> Singular support inside the cone
- **r-simultaneous-cuts** [claim](#stmt-det-simultaneous) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-simultaneous): Incidence variety over projective space and hyperplane-power triangle -> Vanishing for complete-intersection cuts
- **r-specialization-detection** [claim](#stmt-det-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-simultaneous-2): Dimension count for a general plane avoiding the cone, then a finite local piece gives a nonzero stalk -> Closure of generic support misses no point of the special fibre
- **r-scalar-action** [claim](#stmt-perv-scalar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-perv-scalar): Excursion operators computing invariant functions -> Invariant functions act by scalars
- **r-equivariant-frame** [claim](#stmt-perv-frame) / [proof](#proof-stmt-perv-frame): Closed orbit of a dense tuple, equivariant sections, adjugate identities -> Invariant function trivializing each tautological bundle
- **r-fixed-point-exactness** [claim](#stmt-perv-exact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-perv-exact): Naturality for truncations and the frame identity making fixed-point Hecke functors sums of the identity -> Exactness on the localized subcategory
- **r-perverse-eigen** [claim](#stmt-perv-eigen) / [proof](#proof-stmt-perv-exact-2): Moving-leg exactness, truncation comparisons and collision restriction, then adic transfer -> Coherent eigenstructure on perverse cohomology
- **r-monodromic** [claim](#stmt-desc-monodromic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-monodromic): Cone on the enhanced stack is a pullback, then product stratification criterion -> Monodromicity along the torus torsor
- **r-torus-exchange** [claim](#stmt-desc-exchange) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-exchange): Quotient by the radial subgroup to a compact torus bundle and use proper base change -> Exchange of nearby cycles and pushforward
- **r-central-trace** [claim](#stmt-desc-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-trace): Moving correspondence with other levels transported, single-step and two-step comparison squares, local trace formula -> Character equals trace of boundary monodromy
- **r-ordinary-descent** [claim](#stmt-desc-ordinary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-ordinary): Unipotent torus monodromy, nonzero compactly supported cohomology, base change for the Hecke system -> Nonzero perverse eigensheaf on the ordinary-flag stack
- **r-unramified** [claim](#stmt-con-unramified) / [proof](#stmt-con-unramified): Cited correspondence and compact skyscraper object at a dense parameter -> Eigencomplex on the unlevelled stack in characteristic zero
- **r-nodal-quotient** [claim](#stmt-con-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-quotient): Frame calculation on twisted root discs and simultaneous gluing at the nodes -> Special fibre as torus quotient of enhanced stacks
- **r-smoothing-parameter** [claim](#stmt-con-parameter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-parameter): Finite quotients extended to twisted models, glued with inverse boundary actions, lifted by henselian invariance -> Dense unramified parameter on the generic curve
- **r-charzero-eigensheaf** [claim](#stmt-con-charzero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-charzero): Nearby cycles of the unramified eigencomplex, nonvanishing by detection, restriction to an enhanced stack, then descent -> Perverse eigensheaf on the Borel-level stack over the complex numbers
- **r-parameter-lift** [claim](#stmt-lift-parameter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lift-parameter): Lift finite torsors over root stacks of the marked sections and compare inertia -> Lift of the parameter with the same image and inertia
- **r-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-intro-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lift-parameter-2): Take nearby cycles of the characteristic-zero eigensheaf, prove nonvanishing by detection, and transport the eigenisomorphisms -> The eigensheaf of the main theorem

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-existence-of-eigensheaf**: Under the standing hypotheses on the curve, the characteristic and the parameter, a nonzero locally constructible perverse eigensheaf exists on the Borel-level bundle stack. The theorem is purely existential: it gives no finite procedure that produces the sheaf.
- **inv-nonzero-perverse-sheaf**: The sheaf produced is nonzero, locally constructible and perverse, with singular support in the generic nilpotent cone and a full coherent Hecke eigenstructure.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-small-characteristic**: Apply the existence result to curves over fields whose characteristic is at most the rank. -> The construction and its Lie-theoretic inputs are only established in the stated characteristic range; outside it nothing is claimed.
- **anti-single-marked-point**: Use the theorem for a curve with only one marked point or none. -> The several-point degeneration needs at least two marked points, so the statement does not cover fewer.
- **anti-wild-ramification**: Use the theorem for parameters with wild ramification at a marked point. -> Parameters are required to kill wild inertia, so wildly ramified parameters are outside the statement.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main theorem is a purely existential statement about sheaves on bundle stacks; it is built through nearby cycles, descent and categorical correspondences, and gives no finite procedure with explicit steps that a program could run and check against an oracle.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an existence theorem for sheaves on a bundle stack. It gives no finite procedure that a program could execute, so there is no library or API to derive from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are adic complexes on moduli stacks of bundles over curves. They have no state space, dynamics or discretisation that a simulation could use. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper does not give a checker, solver or converter with defined inputs and outputs. Its reductions are proof devices, not executable tool specifications. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is non-constructive: it uses degeneration to a nodal curve, nearby cycles and a contradiction argument for nonvanishing. No explicit algorithm with a complexity bound is stated. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Torsors, stacks and constructible complexes are abstract geometric objects here. The paper defines no finite representation with operations whose cost could be analysed. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no arithmetic kernel, dataflow or numerical workload in the text that an accelerator could implement. The sheaves involved have infinite-dimensional stalks in places. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security reduction appears. The Galois parameters are studied for existence of automorphic sheaves, not as keys or cryptographic objects. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no data, loss, model class or learning guarantee. Its perverse sheaves are not a hypothesis class for statistical learning. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, feasible set or optimality statement occurs. The only dimension-type estimate concerns a cotangent cone and is used inside the proof, not as a bound for a programme. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Several marked points and modifications at multiple legs are geometric structure on a curve. They are not nodes, messages or a consensus model. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only outlet is within pure mathematics: the result serves as a hypothesis-bound input for the geometric Langlands programme. It has no further engineering reading, and the geometric statement does not give a Frobenius structure. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-characteristic-range** (hypothesis): The existence theorem is stated only when the characteristic of the base field exceeds the rank. Nothing is asserted for small characteristic. Blocked use: ['Citing the theorem for curves over fields whose characteristic is at most the rank', 'Treating the Lie-theoretic inputs as valid in small characteristic']
- **limit-marked-points** (hypothesis): At least two distinct marked points are required. The simultaneous nodal degeneration is set up for that case, and the one-point case is handled by an earlier companion result. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to an unramified curve or to a curve with a single marked point']
- **limit-genus** (hypothesis): The curve is smooth projective and geometrically connected, with genus at least two. Blocked use: ['Using the construction for the projective line or for elliptic curves']
- **limit-zariski-density** (hypothesis): The parameter must have Zariski-dense image. Density is used for the closed-orbit frame, for exactness of fixed-point Hecke functors, and for the unramified existence input. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to reducible or abelian local systems', 'Using the result for Eisenstein-type parameters']
- **limit-tame-unipotent-monodromy** (hypothesis): The parameter must kill wild inertia and have local monodromy given by a finite nilpotent exponential at each marked point. Regularity of the nilpotents is not required, but unipotence is essential for descent from enhanced flags. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem for wildly ramified parameters', 'Using the theorem for non-unipotent tame monodromy']
- **limit-nonvanishing-of-nearby-cycles** (edge_case): Specialization is a delicate stage: nearby cycles of a nonzero complex can vanish. Nonvanishing is obtained only through a separate detection argument, which needs the eigenstructure with lisse eigenvalues and the stated Hecke geometry. Blocked use: ['Assuming a specialized eigensheaf is nonzero without checking the detection hypotheses', 'Replacing the detection argument by a generic nonvanishing claim']
- **limit-isotropy-characteristic-zero** (formal_scope): Isotropy of the nilpotent cone is proved only in characteristic zero. In positive characteristic only the dimension bound on charts is available. Blocked use: ['Using isotropy of the cone in positive characteristic', 'Applying the symplectic argument to the characteristic p stack directly']
- **limit-product-quotient-stacks** (formal_scope): The product and quotient stacks of enhanced flags belong to the characteristic-zero degeneration only. They are not available as level stacks in the final positive-characteristic statement. Blocked use: ['Using the nodal quotient description over the original curve in positive characteristic']
- **limit-betti-kernels** (approximation): The universal monodromic kernels with infinite-dimensional stalks live only on the Betti side. They verify properties of constructed adic objects and cannot be used as adic sheaves. Blocked use: ['Treating the monodromic kernels as geometric adic complexes', 'Specializing them with adic nearby cycles']
- **limit-no-frobenius** (nonconstructive): The sheaf is purely geometric and is obtained by non-constructive means (categorical correspondence, nearby cycles, a nonvanishing argument). No Frobenius structure and no explicit procedure for producing the sheaf are given. Blocked use: ['Reading the theorem as an arithmetic statement about Frobenius eigenvalues over a finite field', 'Presenting the construction as an algorithm with a running time']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For SL<sub><i>n</i></sub> in characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; <i>n</i>, we construct nonzero locally constructible perverse Hecke eigensheaves with Borel level at two or more marked points on a smooth projective curve of genus at least two over an algebraic closure of a finite field. The parameter is a Zariski-dense geometric <i>ℓ</i>-adic PGL<sub><i>n</i></sub>-local system with unipotent tame monodromy; its nilpotent logarithms may have any Jordan type, including zero. The eigensheaves have parabolic nilpotent singular support, and their eigenisomorphisms retain the full tensor and fusion structure.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
