<a id="stmt-con-parameter"></a>
<div id="con:parameter" class="lemma">

**Lemma 23**. *Put $K_1=\overline{\mathbb C((z))}$ and let $C_{K_1}$ be the smooth geometric generic curve. There is a continuous unramified parameter $\rho_{K_1}$ on $C_{K_1}$ with the same compact image as $\rho_1$. Its lattice reductions specialize on $U_1\sqcup U_2$ to those of $\rho_1$ and $\rho_2$, with the tensor compatibilities of Proposition <a href="#found:hecke-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:hecke-specialization">4</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-con-parameter"><summary>Proof of stmt-con-parameter</summary>

*Proof.* Let $H=\rho_1(\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(U_1))\subset\check G(L')$ be the compact image, for a finite coefficient field $L'$. Choose a cofinal decreasing sequence of open normal subgroups $H_r$ and put $Q_r=H/H_r$. One obtains such a sequence from congruence kernels of an $H$-stable lattice in a faithful representation. Both $U_j$ carry connected $Q_r$-torsors. Choose indices $m_r$ divisible by the fixed $m$ and by the orders of all their boundary images, with $m_r\mid m_{r+1}$.

By the root-stack description of tame covers <a id="pm-cite-88"></a>[[LieblichOlsson](#ref-LieblichOlsson), Proposition A.10], the torsors extend to finite étale torsors on the normalization of $C(m_r)_0$. At each pair of gerbes their actions agree: the node identifies a positive generator on the first branch with a negative generator on the second, and <a href="#con:boundary-inversion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="con:boundary-inversion">[con:boundary-inversion]</a> cancels that inversion. Reduce the chosen boundary-fiber identifications modulo $H_r$ to identify these restrictions. They are compatible at every level of the tower. The torsors glue to a finite étale $Q_r$-torsor $Y_{r,0}$ on $C(m_r)_0$. Locally this is nodal gluing of finite étale algebras with identified fibers: on the completed branch discs the covers are constant, with the same $\mu_{m_r}$-action at the origin, so their gluing is constant on the nodal cover and descends equivariantly. This also proves compatibility under the power maps of the twisted models.

<a id="con:generic-homomorphism"></a>
Proper henselian invariance of finite étale covers lifts $Y_{r,0}$ to a torsor $Y_r$ over $C(m_r)$ <a id="pm-cite-89"></a>[[Rydh](#ref-Rydh), Theorem 4.5]. Its hypotheses hold because the models are proper with finite diagonal over complete henselian traits. Full faithfulness lifts the group actions, transition maps after pullback to divisible models, and all relations between them. All generic fibers become $C_{K_1}$. Choose a base section specializing in $U_1$ and compatible points of the torsor fibers above it; finite étale covers of this strictly henselian section are constant. The tower therefore gives a continuous homomorphism $$\label{con:generic-homomorphism}
 \rho_{K_1}:\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(C_{K_1})\longrightarrow
                      \varprojlim_r Q_r=H.$$

We check its image. Each $Y_{r,0}$ is connected, since its two normalization torsors are connected and are glued along nonempty fibers. The total torsor $Y_r$ is proper and flat with reduced geometric fibers. Its tame coarse space is flat as well: locally its coordinate ring is the $\mu_{m_r}$-invariant direct summand of an $R_{m_r}$-flat ring, and invariant formation commutes with base change. The connected reduced proper special fiber has $H^0(\mathcal O)=\mathbb C$. Semicontinuity, applied to the coarse space, gives $h^0(Y_{r,K_1},\mathcal O)=1$, so the geometric generic torsor is connected. Thus <a href="#con:generic-homomorphism" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="con:generic-homomorphism">[con:generic-homomorphism]</a> surjects onto every $Q_r$. Its image is compact and therefore closed in $H$; surjectivity to this cofinal system makes it all of $H$. In particular the parameter remains Zariski dense.

For an algebraic representation of $\check G$, enlarge its finite coefficient field if necessary and choose an $H$-stable lattice. Each finite reduction factors through some $Q_r$. Its lifted torsor supplies a lisse extension over the smooth leg locus after the corresponding finite trait extension, with the specified special reductions on both components. All twisted models agree away from the nodes. The tower thus gives the required tensor compatibilities; maps between lattices are compared after clearing denominators. As allowed in Proposition <a href="#found:hecke-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:hecke-specialization">4</a>, different reductions may require different finite trait extensions. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-32"></a>
#### A nonzero specialization and descent

Lemma <a href="#con:unramified" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="con:unramified">21</a> now supplies a nonzero locally bounded constructible eigencomplex $F$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(C_{K_1})$ with parameter $\rho_{K_1}$. Take its geometric nearby cycles on the fixed model $\mathcal B_m$. Propositions <a href="#found:nearby" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:nearby">3</a> and <a href="#found:hecke-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:hecke-specialization">4</a>, with the geometry and lattice extensions just verified, give a locally bounded constructible eigencomplex $\Psi F$ on $(\mathcal B_m)_0$, for legs on both $U_1$ and $U_2$. To use it we must prove nonvanishing on precisely this prescribed-type fiber.

Choose a point of the product in <a href="#con:special-quotient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="con:special-quotient">[con:special-quotient]</a> and lift its image to a reference bundle $P_0$ over $R_m$. This is possible by smoothness of $\mathcal B_m$: lift a point in a smooth chart over the henselian trait. Choose also a section $y$ of the smooth scheme locus, disjoint from the nodes. Since $F$ is nonzero, some $K_1$-valued bundle $P$ has nonzero stalk. Both $P$ and $(P_0)_{K_1}$ are trivial on the affine curve $C_{K_1}\setminus\{y_{K_1}\}$. Indeed, a rank-$n$ projective module over a Dedekind domain is the sum of a free rank-$(n-1)$ module and its determinant; an $\mathrm{SL}_n$-bundle has trivial determinant, and its frame can be adjusted to respect the chosen determinant trivialization.

An isomorphism off $y_{K_1}$ exhibits $P$ as a modification of $(P_0)_{K_1}$ in a finite Schubert bound. Its point is defined over a finite extension of the trait’s fraction field. Properness of the bounded Hecke space over the fixed input $P_0$ and leg $y$ extends this modification over the resulting trait. Its output still has the prescribed types, because the modification is away from all nodes. Lift this section to a finite-type smooth chart through its special value. Its generic stalk is the chosen nonzero stalk, so the closure of the generic nonzero-stalk locus on this chart meets the special fiber. Theorem <a href="#det:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="det:specialization">10</a> therefore gives <a id="con:nonzero-nearby"></a>$$\label{con:nonzero-nearby}
                         \Psi F\ne0.$$ Here finite extensions cause no change to geometric nearby cycles, as in Proposition <a href="#found:nearby" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:nearby">3</a>.

Pull back $\Psi F$ ordinarily along the smooth surjection $\mathcal A_1^+\times\mathcal A_2^+\to(\mathcal B_m)_0$. Its pullback is nonzero. By local constructibility choose a $\mathbb C$-valued point $(a_1,a_2)$ with nonzero stalk and restrict to $\mathcal A_1^+\times\{a_2\}$. This gives a nonzero locally bounded constructible complex $F_1^+$ on $\mathcal A_1^+$. By Lemma <a href="#con:quotient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="con:quotient">22</a>, the first component’s Hecke correspondences are the base changes of those downstairs and act on the first factor alone. Proper base change on each bound makes both pullbacks compatible with the full coherent Hecke system. Thus $F_1^+$ has eigenvalue $\sigma$. These are ordinary pullbacks; perversity will be obtained after descent.

<a id="stmt-con-charzero"></a>
<div id="con:charzero" class="proposition">

**Proposition 24**. *Let $X/\mathbb C$ be a smooth projective connected curve of genus at least two, with $s\geq2$ distinct marked points, divisor $D$ their sum, and $G=\mathrm{SL}_n$. Let $\sigma$ be a continuous dense $\check G$-parameter on their complement, defined over a finite coefficient extension, with unipotent monodromy at every marking. Then $\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_{G,B,D}(X)$ carries a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric $E$-adic eigensheaf with eigenvalue $\sigma$, nilpotent singular support, and the full coherent Hecke system in relative Satake normalization.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-con-charzero"><summary>Proof of stmt-con-charzero</summary>

*Proof.* The construction gives $F_1^+$ on the enhanced stack for $X$. Proposition <a href="#desc:ordinary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:ordinary">20</a> applies because the parameter is dense and each boundary monodromy is unipotent, and gives a nonzero perverse eigenobject on the ordinary-flag stack. Its singular support lies in the required cone by Theorem <a href="#det:field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="det:field">9</a>. No regularity of any local nilpotent is used. ◻

</details>

<a id="lift:section"></a>
### Lifting the parameter and specializing to characteristic $p$

We return to the curve and parameter of Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a>. The remaining step is to lift the marked curve and its parameter to characteristic zero, apply Proposition <a href="#con:charzero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="con:charzero">24</a>, and specialize the resulting perverse eigensheaf. We retain the full inertia homomorphisms in the lift, including when some $N_i$ vanish.

Let $R=W(k)$. This is an excellent complete strictly henselian discrete valuation ring, and $\ell$ is invertible in $R$. There is a smooth projective lift with disjoint marked sections $$(\mathscr X,\mathfrak x_1,\ldots,\mathfrak x_s)
                         \longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$$ of $(X,x_1,\ldots,x_s)$. Indeed, the obstruction group for the marked curve is $H^2(X,T_X(-D))=0$; an ample line bundle also lifts, and formal algebraization gives the projective family. Use the split models of $G$ and $B$ over $R$, and put $$\mathscr D=\sum_i\mathfrak x_i,\qquad
 \mathscr U=\mathscr X\setminus|\mathscr D|,\qquad
 K_0=\overline{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}.$$

<a id="stmt-lift-parameter"></a>
<div id="lift:parameter" class="lemma">

<a id="lift:inertia"></a>
**Lemma 25**. *The parameter $\rho$ lifts to a continuous parameter $\rho_{K_0}:\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(\mathscr U_{K_0})\to\check G(L)$ with the same compact image. After compatible choices of boundary fibers and prime-to-$p$ roots of unity, its full inertia homomorphism at $\mathfrak x_i$ is $$\label{lift:inertia}
 \rho_{K_0}(\gamma)=\exp\bigl(t_{\ell,i}(\gamma)N_i\bigr).$$ In particular every other prime factor of characteristic-zero inertia acts trivially. Its lattice reductions extend lisse over $\mathscr U$ and specialize to those of $\rho$, with the tensor compatibilities of Proposition <a href="#found:hecke-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:hecke-specialization">4</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lift-parameter"><summary>Proof of stmt-lift-parameter</summary>

*Proof.* We use the finite-cover lifting argument of <a id="pm-cite-90"></a>[[CT](#ref-CT), Section 8.5, Lemma 8.4], applied at all the disjoint markings. Let $H=\rho(\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(U))$, choose a cofinal system $Q_r=H/H_r$ as in Lemma <a href="#con:parameter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="con:parameter">23</a>, and let $Y_r^\circ$ be the corresponding connected finite étale torsor on $U$. At every marking its inertia image has $\ell$-power order, since the given inertia homomorphism factors through $\mathbb Z_\ell$. Choose $d_r=\ell^{e_r}$ divisible by all these orders at stage $r$, with $d_r\mid d_{r+1}$. The torsor extends to the proper root stack $$X_r=X\bigl(\sqrt[d_r]{x_1},\ldots,\sqrt[d_r]{x_s}\bigr).$$ This is the root-stack description of tame covers <a id="pm-cite-91"></a>[[LieblichOlsson](#ref-LieblichOlsson), Proposition A.10]. The corresponding relative root stack $$\mathscr X_r=
 \mathscr X\bigl(\sqrt[d_r]{\mathfrak x_1},\ldots,
                         \sqrt[d_r]{\mathfrak x_s}\bigr)$$ is smooth, proper, and tame with finite diagonal over $R$, since $d_r$ is invertible in $R$.

Proper henselian invariance lifts the finite étale torsors to $\mathscr X_r$ <a id="pm-cite-92"></a>[[Rydh](#ref-Rydh), Theorem 4.5]; full faithfulness lifts their group actions, transition maps after pullback to divisible root stacks, and all compatibility relations. See also <a id="pm-cite-93"></a>[[LieblichOlsson](#ref-LieblichOlsson), Theorem A.11 and Corollaries A.12–A.13]. Restricting to $\mathscr U$ and then to its geometric generic fiber, and choosing compatible base points, produces $$\rho_{K_0}:\pi_1^{\mathrm{et}}(\mathscr U_{K_0})\longrightarrow H.$$ Only the root indices must be prime to $p$; the finite group orders $|Q_r|$ need not be.

The special torsor on $X_r$ is connected: its inverse image of $U$ is dense and is the connected torsor $Y_r^\circ$. It is smooth and geometrically reduced. The proper flat tame-coarse-space argument in Lemma <a href="#con:parameter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="con:parameter">23</a> gives connected geometric generic root-stack torsors. A smooth connected curve stack is irreducible; removing the inverse images of the finitely many marked gerbes therefore leaves it connected. Thus $\rho_{K_0}$ surjects onto every $Q_r$, and its compact image is all of $H$. In particular the lifted parameter is Zariski dense.

To determine inertia, trivialize the normal line of each marked section over the strictly henselian trait. Its root gerbes are then $B\mu_{d_r}$, with compatible atlases as $r$ varies. Restricting the lifted torsor to this gerbe and pulling to its atlas gives a constant finite torsor together with its $\mu_{d_r}$-action. Full faithfulness of lifting retains exactly the special-fiber action. Compatible prime-to-$p$ roots of unity identify the generic and special generators. One may choose the boundary frames compatibly throughout the tower: the possible frames form an inverse system of nonempty finite fibers with surjective transition maps. At every stage, generic inertia consequently factors through $\mu_{d_r}$ and agrees with the original inertia map. Passing to the inverse limit proves <a href="#lift:inertia" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lift:inertia">[lift:inertia]</a> for the whole inertia group. In particular its $\mathbb Z_a(1)$ factor acts trivially for every prime $a\ne\ell$, including $a=p$. This records the full peripheral compatibility, rather than only the conjugacy class of one monodromy element; compare <a id="pm-cite-94"></a>[[LieblichOlsson](#ref-LieblichOlsson), Lemma 3.5].

Finally, each finite reduction of an $H$-stable lattice in an algebraic representation factors through some $Q_r$. The lifted torsor restricted to $\mathscr U$ is lisse and has the required special fiber. Tensor products and maps between the resulting systems come from the same tower, with denominators cleared when comparing lattices. This proves the asserted lattice extension condition. ◻

</details>

We may identify $K_0$ abstractly with $\mathbb C$. Indeed $k$ is countable and $W(k)$ is a set of sequences in $k$, while it contains $\mathbb Z_p$. Its cardinality, and hence that of $K_0$, is the continuum. An uncountable algebraically closed characteristic-zero field has transcendence degree equal to its cardinality, so $K_0$ and $\mathbb C$ are isomorphic. This identification is only of the geometric base fields; the adic topology of the coefficient field and continuity of the parameter remain unchanged.

<details id="proof-stmt-lift-parameter-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lift-parameter</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a>.* <span id="lift:main-proof" label="lift:main-proof"></span> By Lemma <a href="#lift:parameter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lift:parameter">25</a> and Proposition <a href="#con:charzero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="con:charzero">24</a>, the ordinary-flag bundle stack over $K_0$ has a nonzero locally constructible perverse eigenobject $M_{K_0}$ with eigenvalue $\rho_{K_0}$. Put $$\mathscr A=\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_{G,B,\mathscr D}(\mathscr X/R),\qquad
                  M=\Psi M_{K_0}\quad\text{on }\mathscr A_k=\mathcal A.$$ The stack $\mathscr A$ is smooth over $R$: the unlevelled bundle stack is smooth by vanishing of the degree-two obstruction groups, and its flag fibers are products of the smooth variety $G/B$. Proposition <a href="#found:nearby" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:nearby">3</a> implies that $M$ is locally constructible and perverse. Thus for a smooth finite-type chart $f:S\to\mathcal A$ of relative dimension $d$, the complex $f^*M[d]$ is bounded constructible and perverse. These are geometric nearby cycles, without taking inertia invariants.

<a id="lift:final-eigenmaps"></a>
The Hecke geometry on $\mathscr U$ is the split smooth-leg geometry of Section <a href="#found:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:section">2</a>. The lattice extensions in Lemma <a href="#lift:parameter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lift:parameter">25</a> and Proposition <a href="#found:hecke-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:hecke-specialization">4</a> give $$\label{lift:final-eigenmaps}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{I,(V_i)}(M)\simeq
 M\boxtimes\Bigl(\boxtimes_{i\in I}(V_i)_\rho\Bigr)
 \qquad\text{on }\mathcal A\times U^I.$$ This is the relative Satake normalization $E[d_\lambda](d_\lambda/2)$, with no moving-leg shift. The isomorphisms are natural in all $V_i$ and retain the unit, convolution, permutation, and fusion compatibilities on every collision diagonal, since specialization transports the coherent system itself.

It remains to show that $M$ is nonzero. Local constructibility gives a $K_0$-valued point $(P,(\beta_i))$ of the geometric generic bundle stack at which $M_{K_0}$ has nonzero stalk. Choose a reference bundle $P_0$ on $\mathscr X$, for example the trivial bundle. A point of $U$ lifts to a section $y$ of $\mathscr U$ by smoothness and henselianity. On the affine curve $\mathscr X_{K_0}\setminus\{y_{K_0}\}$, both $P$ and $(P_0)_{K_0}$ are trivial by the determinant argument used in Section <a href="#con:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="con:section">7</a>. Hence $P$ is a modification of $(P_0)_{K_0}$ at $y_{K_0}$ in some finite Schubert bound. After a finite trait extension defining the data, properness of the bounded unlevelled Hecke space extends the modification and thus the bundle. Each flag $\beta_i$ then extends by properness of the associated $G/B$-bundle over the marked section. We have extended the original point to the relative ordinary-flag stack.

Lift this section to a finite-type smooth chart through its special value, after a further henselian lift if necessary. Its generic stalk is still nonzero, so the closure of the generic nonzero-stalk locus meets the special fiber on that chart. Theorem <a href="#det:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="det:specialization">10</a> forces $\Psi M_{K_0}\ne0$. Its hypotheses hold by the Hecke geometry above, the lattice extensions of Lemma <a href="#lift:parameter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lift:parameter">25</a>, and the special-fiber geometry of Section <a href="#geom:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:section">3</a>. Geometric nearby cycles are unchanged by the finite trait extensions used in this argument.

Finally, Theorem <a href="#det:field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="det:field">9</a>, applied to <a href="#lift:final-eigenmaps" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lift:final-eigenmaps">[lift:final-eigenmaps]</a>, gives $\relax(M)\subset\Lambda$ on every smooth chart. At ordinary flag level, Definition <a href="#geom:cone" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:cone">5</a> and its cotangent description identify this with the cone of generically nilpotent Higgs fields $$\phi\in H^0\bigl(X,\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}(P)\otimes\omega_X(D)\bigr),\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{x_i}\phi\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}R_u(B_{\beta_i})\quad(1\leq i\leq s).$$ The condition $p>n$ gives precisely the characteristic hypotheses used in that geometry and detection. The nonzero sheaf $M$ has all the asserted properties. Every parameter and sheaf used in the construction is geometric; no Frobenius structure is required. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The cartesian square of two modifications with a common output and leg. The cut is imposed through the left-hand map to $D$; the coefficient is pulled from the first input at the upper right. The diagonal section $W_{0} → P$ makes the two modifications equal.",
      "figure_id": "det:figure",
      "pdf_page": 15,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "det:figure",
          "exact_quote": "The cartesian square of two modifications with a common output\nand leg. The cut is imposed through the left-hand map to $D$; the\ncoefficient is pulled from the first input at the upper right.\nThe diagonal section $W_0\\to P$ makes the two mo",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Stealth,baseline=(current bounding box.center)]\n\\node (D) at (0,0) {$D$};\n\\node (W) at (3,0) {$W_0$};\n\\node (P) at (3,1.8) {$P$};\n\\node (H) at (6.2,1.8) {$H_1$};\n\\node (O) at (6.2,0) {$\\cB\\times L$};\n\\node (B) at (9,1.8) {$\\cB$};\n\\draw[->] (P) -- node[above] {$\\operatorname{pr}_1$} (H);\n\\draw[->] (P) -- node[left] {$\\operatorname{pr}_2$} (W);\n\\draw[->] (H) -- node[right] {$q_{H,1}$} (O);\n\\draw[->] (W) -- node[below] {$q_{H,2}$} (O);\n\\draw[->] (W) -- node[below] {\\small second input} (D);\n\\draw[->] (H) -- node[above] {$p_1$} (B);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Commutative diagram with nodes and arrows:\n- Top row: node $P$, arrow pointing right to node $H_1$ labeled $\\mathrm{pr}_1$, followed by arrow pointing right to node $B$ labeled $p_1$.\n- Middle/bottom row: node $D$, arrow pointing left from node $W_0$ to $D$ with text \"second input\" below the arrow; arrow pointing right from node $W_0$ to node $B \\times L$ labeled $q_{H,2}$.\n- Vertical arrows: arrow pointing down from $P$ to $W_0$ labeled $\\mathrm{pr}_2$; arrow pointing down from $H_1$ to $B \\times L$ labeled $q_{H,1}$."
    },
    {
      "caption": "Normalization and gluing at all $s$ nodes (schematically). The ovals are the coarse curves of the normalized root stacks. The dashed pairs are identified, not extra curve components. For bundle types the gerbes have fixed order $m$ and each pair contributes one frame-change torus to the displayed quotient. For parameter covers at stage $r$, replace $m$ by $m_{r}$ and $ρ_{j}$ by its finite quotient at that stage. The inverse boundary actions then agree on the gluing gerbe, because its common generator acts oppositely on the two branch coordinates.",
      "figure_id": "con:gluing-figure",
      "pdf_page": 34,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "con:gluing-figure",
          "exact_quote": "Normalization and gluing at all $s$ nodes (schematically).\nThe ovals are the coarse curves of the normalized root stacks.\nThe dashed pairs are identified, not extra curve components.\nFor bundle types the gerbes have fixed order $m$ and each",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,>=Stealth,font=\\small]\n \\draw[thick] (-2.5,0) ellipse (1.0 and 1.6);\n \\draw[thick] (2.5,0) ellipse (1.0 and 1.6);\n \\node at (-2.65,0) {$X_1$};\n \\node at (2.65,0) {$X_2$};\n \\foreach \\yy/\\ii in {1/1,-1/s} {\n   \\fill (-1.72,\\yy) circle (1.6pt);\n   \\fill (1.72,\\yy) circle (1.6pt);\n   \\draw[dashed] (-1.72,\\yy)--(1.72,\\yy);\n   \\node[above] at (-1.32,\\yy) {$x_{1\\ii}$};\n   \\node[above] at (1.32,\\yy) {$x_{2\\ii}$};\n   \\node[fill=white,inner sep=2pt] at (0,\\yy) {$B\\mu_m$};\n }\n \\node at (0,0) {$\\vdots$};\n \\node[align=center] at (-2.5,-2.1)\n      {positive branch $u$\\\\enhanced $B$-flags};\n \\node[align=center] at (2.5,-2.1)\n      {negative branch $v$\\\\enhanced $B^-$-flags};\n \\draw[->,thick] (1.5,2.05) to[bend right=12]\n       node[above,align=center] {$f$ reverses orientation\\\\at every boundary}\n       (-1.5,2.05);\n \\node[align=center] at (0,-3.12)\n      {$\\zeta:(u,v)\\mapsto(\\zeta u,\\zeta^{-1}v)$\\qquad\n       $\\rho_2(c_{2i})=\\rho_1(c_{1i})^{-1}$\\\\[3pt]\n       $\\displaystyle (\\cB_m)_0\\simeq\n         [(\\cA_1^+\\times\\cA_2^+)/T^s]$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Schematic diagram showing:\n- Two large vertical ovals representing coarse curves labeled $X_1$ (left) and $X_2$ (right).\n- On the right boundary of $X_1$, points $x_{11}$ (top) and $x_{1s}$ (bottom) with vertical dots $\\vdots$ in between.\n- On the left boundary of $X_2$, points $x_{21}$ (top) and $x_{2s}$ (bottom) with vertical dots $\\vdots$ in between.\n- Dashed horizontal lines connecting $x_{11}$ to $x_{21}$ (labeled above with $B\\mu_m$) and $x_{1s}$ to $x_{2s}$ (labeled below with $B\\mu_m$), with vertical dots $\\vdots$ in between the dashed lines.\n- Above the curves, a curved arrow spanning over the top with label \"$f$ reverses orientation\" above it and \"at every boundary\" below it.\n- Under oval $X_1$, text: \"positive branch $u$\" and \"enhanced $B$-flags\".\n- Under oval $X_2$, text: \"negative branch $v$\" and \"enhanced $B^-$-flags\".\n- Below these labels, mathematical relations: $\\zeta : (u, v) \\mapsto (\\zeta u, \\zeta^{-1} v)$, $\\rho_2(c_{2i}) = \\rho_1(c_{1i})^{-1}$, and $(B_m)_0 \\simeq [(A_1^+ \\times A_2^+) / T^s]$."
    }
  ],
